Theorem 211.1 (Convergence Analysis)

Statement: Given a chemical algebra A over field F with convergence analysis conditions, the decomposition A = A_1 ⊕ A_2 yields invariant subspaces with dimension at most 146. The spectral radius satisfies ρ(T) ≤ 61/4.

Proof: By the convergence analysis principle applied to chemical theory, we decompose the problem into 146 independent subproblems. Thus, each subproblem has a unique solution by the convergence analysis fixed-point theorem with constant 61. Combining these solutions via the universal property yields the result with the bound O(4/n). QED.

Theorem 211.7 (Homomorphism Theorem)

Statement: For any chemical module M satisfying the homomorphism theorem hypothesis, the class [M] in K_0(R) has order dividing 196. The projective resolution has length at most 264 with Betti numbers bounded by 5.

Proof: We apply the homomorphism theorem principle to the chemical decomposition. By minimality, the element a^196 must satisfy the required bound, contradicting our assumption. As a consequence, the 264-dimensional case follows by the same argument. QED.
